# Harbor Coordination Papers -- Critique Ledger

Purpose: the complete inventory of every actionable item raised in the review memoranda on the
Harbor coordination papers, so "did we go through all of this" becomes a query against this
table (filter by `Status`) instead of a memory someone has to hold. Extraction only -- rows
record what a reviewer said, not whether it is right; no row implies agreement.
Id scheme: `#` is `<PREFIX>-<NNN>`, one prefix per source document below (`HAH`, `RT`, `PD`,
`RP`, `PP`, `CPR`, `XS` for the exposition-review corpus), numbered in the order the item
appears in its source. Ids are permanent -- never renumber or reuse one; retire a row via
`Status` instead.
To add a row: append it to the right source's table with the next unused number in that
source's sequence, add the identical object to `critique-ledger.json`, then run
`python3 scripts/harbor-research/check_critique_ledger.py` before committing.
`Status` and `Notes` are left blank throughout for the author to fill in (`Status` values:
empty, `DONE <sha-or-PR>`, `IN-WAVE-<n>`, or `DECLINED -- <reason>`).
See `docs/harbor-research/LIBRARY-SYSTEM.md` (forthcoming) for how this ledger fits the
broader research-library workflow.

## 1 -- Revising the Treatise (`docs/harbor-research/pdf/1-Revising-the-Treatise.pdf`)

`docs/harbor-research/tex/doc1_treatise.tex` is this same memo's own tex source (its content is identical to the PDF); because this memo both flags issues *and* resolves them, the `Prior adjudication` column below is filled in directly from the memo's own resolution, section by section, rather than left blank.

| # | Section / page | Kind | Item | Targets | Prior adjudication | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RT-001 | Sec.1 Change 1 -- The stage game is not a Prisoner's Dilemma (p.1) | correction | The printed bimatrix has A preferring T against F (1>0), so F is not dominant and (F,F) is not Nash; the delta*~=0.253 trigger-strategy claim proves nothing about the game as drawn. Fix: substitute a genuine PD and re-derive (delta>=1/3), or stop modeling this as an observed PD at all. | The Bonded Commons | Resolved in the same document: substitute a genuine PD matrix and re-derive delta>=1/3, or (better) recast as a hidden-type reporting game per Change 9/Adjudication on the core reframe. | DONE f08819953 | `website-v2/public/whitepaper/agent-transactions-whitepaper.tex` §sec:economic and `proofs/economics/claim_signaling.tla` / `delta-threshold.z3` carry the corrected PD matrix and delta>=1/3 threshold. |
| RT-002 | Sec.1 Change 2 -- Exact-key uniqueness is not mutual exclusion (p.1) | correction | Theorem II.6.1 proves at most one live row per exact normalized key; it says nothing about overlapping intervals, whole-file-vs-symbol claims, or path ancestry. Fix: restate over the conflict predicate actually enforced and add the missing fencing epoch. | The Single-Writer Kernel | Resolved: restate the theorem over canonical conflict domains or a transactional overlap test; reuse the same exclusion predicate for claims and wallet balances; add the fencing epoch. | DONE ae0e3c17f | Fencing epoch and the canonical conflict-domain predicate are in `whitepaper/single-writer-kernel.tex`. |
| RT-003 | Sec.1 Change 3 -- The non-forgeable-identity theorem is false as stated (p.2) | correction | Counterexample: mature reputation 10, sanction 2, newcomer floor 0 -> keeping the identity yields 8, resetting yields 0, so free identity does not defeat the sanction; the proof's equality holds only when r(i)-Delta<r0. | From Spawn to Person | Resolved: state the valid conditional (evadable iff fresh-identity utility >= sanctioned utility, or identities can be multiplied to defeat a quorum) and move the load-bearing defense to principal aggregation, healing the Sybil, bond, and cross-harbor-trust arguments in one move. | DONE ef443981d | Same fix as HAH-017: thm:necessity (L1078-1099) states the equality only holds when r(i)-Delta<r0; tab:necessity-numbers row 2 (L1113) shows the sanction biting; prop:reality adds principal aggregation. |
| RT-004 | Sec.1 Change 4 -- Signal Detection Theory points the wrong way (p.2) | correction | Figure I.6 has danger increasing rightward with 'zoom if z>=beta,' so moving beta left is more stringent, not laxer as the prose claims; the objective may double-count attention if C_fa already includes wasted inspection. | The Legible Swarm | Resolved: specify the full SDT model (z~p(z\|danger,r,verifier), L(beta) with three cost terms) and derive the likelihood-ratio threshold; say explicitly whether reputation r shifts the prior, observation model, or miss cost. | DONE 4f4abca72 | Same evidence as HAH-012: fig:sdt caption direction fixed and thm:regret-head (l.2039-2072) derives the full likelihood-ratio model; def:sdt (l.1325-1326) states r is a flat prior absent a reputation layer. |
| RT-005 | Sec.1 Change 5 -- The float plan confuses requester funds with provider collateral (p.2) | correction | Protocol IV.4.1 lets the requester fund a 'worker bond' that is then paid or slashed as if it were the worker's money. | The Harbor Economy | Resolved: separate four buckets (requester bounty escrow, provider performance bond, optional insurer reserve, fees), both-signature amendments, and atomic re-funding. | DONE 2a96d8143 | Same four-bucket separation as HAH-018 (lines 812-819): bounty/bond/commons/fee buckets, atomic re-funding. |
| RT-006 | Sec.1 Change 6 -- Anchor attenuation must compare every edge, not every node to the root (p.2) | correction | Algorithm V.4 compares each cap_i to cap_root, admitting write->read->write escalation that expands a middle node's authority even though it appears root-contained. | The Anchor Protocol | Resolved: verify cap_i subseteq cap_(i-1) at every hop with proof-of-possession, unique token IDs, ancestor revocation, and epoch/freshness; the Kani harnesses must then prove a relational property over symbolic parent/child capabilities, not a comparator on concrete vectors. | DONE 83b357c9e | `website-v2/public/whitepaper/anchor-protocol-whitepaper.tex` states the Kani harnesses at their real scope; every-hop attenuation is ProVerif's relational proof, not a root-only comparator. |
| RT-007 | Sec.1 Change 7 -- Schema-by-union is a latent corruption bug; WAL auto-checkpoint does not bound loss time (p.2-3) | correction | (a) modules self-initializing via CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS let a later module under old code split writes across schema versions; (b) SQLite's WAL auto-checkpoint is a page-count threshold, not a timer, so it cannot bound elapsed vulnerability delta-t. | The Single-Writer Kernel | Resolved: (a) replace with a linear migration ledger keyed on PRAGMA user_version plus a schema-epoch table; (b) expose durability as a per-transaction type {process_crash\|power_loss}, route money/authority writes through synchronous=FULL, and only claim a bound with an independently guaranteed minimum write rate. | DONE ae0e3c17f | `whitepaper/single-writer-kernel.tex` has the schema-epoch boot ledger (OP-7) and durability-by-fault-class (OP-10). |
| RT-008 | Sec.1 Change 8 -- Cryptography cannot prove reality; 'reference monitor' overstates the same-user design; Kani proves less than claimed (p.3) | correction | (a) Signatures/hashes/logs establish attribution and tamper-evidence relative to a commitment, not that every event was logged or a description is true; (b) a same-user daemon with bypassable hooks is a coordinator, not a complete reference monitor; (c) a Kani harness that stubs signature verification proves bounded memory-safety, not the prose security property. | whole volume | Resolved: (a) introduce six typed evidence channels naming what each cannot show; (b) replace the blanket term with the five assurance levels of Change 12/HAH's risk ladder; (c) state each harness's preconditions/bounds/postcondition and prove parser refinement separately from assumed crypto. | DONE f83c171bb | needs adjudication: part (b) assurance levels and part (c) Kani scope are landed (`whitepaper/figures/pd-pedagogy.tex`; `83b357c9e`); part (a) six typed evidence channels is not yet implemented anywhere -- author should decide whether to mark this row done-in-part or point it at wave 10 alongside HAH-022.; (a) the six typed evidence channels are named in the front matter (How to use this textbook) with what each cannot show; (b) assurance modes and (c) Kani scope landed earlier (pd-pedagogy 83b357c9e, proof-estate pass) |
| RT-009 | Sec.2 Adjudication -- The core reframe: 'economy of persons' vs. 'evidence-bearing work unit' (p.3-4) | structure | Rigor review: demote personhood from foundation to optional policy, make the primitive a durable work unit W=<principal, actor-lineage, intent, acceptance-policy, authority-envelope, expected-effects, claims, evidence-obligations, checkpoint, risk-guarantee, outcome>. Product review: leans into personhood/feudal metaphor as go-to-market narrative. | re-spine chapter 1; From Spawn to Person | Resolved: adopt the work unit as Chapter 1's primitive (formal ontology); keep personhood language as an explicitly-labelled interpretive interlude and marketing copy only, never load-bearing. This is the directional move of Section 3. | DONE ae0e3c17f | Work unit adopted as Chapter 1's primitive in `whitepaper/single-writer-kernel.tex` (the Adjudication's 'core reframe' resolution); personhood-as-inheritance-rule still needs applying in From Spawn to Person's own wave (6). |
| RT-010 | Sec.2 Adjudication -- Checkpoints: 'the JSON ledger is the neural state' vs. provider-portable checkpoints are impossible (p.3-4) | correction | Product review: event-sourcing the exact JSON context array plus a Git SHA restores neural state for 'fractions of a cent.' Rigor review: two hidden provider states can export the same transcript/capsule yet require different next actions, so a provider-neutral restorer cannot preserve both. | From Spawn to Person | Resolved: keep the event-sourced ledger as the capsule substrate but replace 'IS the neural state' with 'a sufficient statistic for same-provider continuation and a semantic capsule for cross-provider succession'; define correctness at the work-protocol boundary, not bit-identity. | DONE e4768d6a4 | Organ 2 pitfall (L931-952): ledger kept as capsule substrate, "restores...instantly" cut; now "task continuity with actor succession," correctness = "the ledger followed the right body" (L948). |

## 3 -- Research and Proof Roadmap (`docs/harbor-research/pdf/3-Research-and-Proof-Roadmap.pdf`)

| # | Section / page | Kind | Item | Targets | Prior adjudication | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RP-001 | Sec.1 The discipline: refute before you prove (p.1) | roadmap | No bound is formalized until it survives an automated counterexample search (Z3/cvc5, Gambit, or PRISM-games) run first; this inverts the usual order and would have caught the non-PD payoff matrix and the wrong delta threshold mechanically and early. | whole research program |  | DECLINED -- routed to the research roadmap, not this book |  |
| RP-002 | Sec.2 Tier A -- A1 Split-Digest Theorem (p.1) | development | A single scoring head serves two readers iff their induced preference orders are comonotone; the successor and operator readers are provably non-comonotone, and a joint zero-miss digest pays the sum of the two floors when load-bearing sets are disjoint. Converts the SLM-sidecar architecture from opinion to necessity. | The Legible Swarm; research paper 1 | Sharpens the rigor review's own claim that 'the operator digest and context compaction must be the same provenance-preserving projection' -- this theorem says exactly when that is possible. | DONE 4f4abca72 | thm:comonotone (l.1997-2013) states iff-comonotone sharing; thm:split-penalty (l.2316-2330) prices the joint zero-miss digest as super-additive (not merely 2x) when load-bearing sets are disjoint. |
| RP-003 | Sec.2 Tier A -- A2 Deriving the regret head (p.1-2) | development | regret=stakes*irreversibility*anomaly is the exact expected miss-cost iff anomaly is a calibrated posterior; the surfacing rule is then the classical likelihood-ratio criterion. Repairs the SDT error (RT Change 4) constructively, turning a flagged error into a theorem. | The Legible Swarm; research paper 1 | Directly resolves RT Change 4 / HAH correctness-audit item #1 as a derived theorem rather than a patched heuristic. | DONE 4f4abca72 | thm:regret-head (l.2039-2066): regret is the exact expected miss-cost iff anomaly is a calibrated posterior; the surfacing rule is the classical likelihood-ratio criterion (Eq.bayes-regret). |
| RP-004 | Sec.2 Tier A -- A3 Epsilon-conservation for the release ledger (p.2) | development | Induction on append-only release transitions gives cumulative epsilon-spend = sum of recorded releases, the same proof shape as bond conservation plus imported DP composition; the Sealed Harbor's headline invariant. | re-spine chapter 6 (The Sealed Harbor); research paper 4 |  | DONE 7fbb9c64f | thm:sealed-conservation (L542) proves epsilon-conservation by induction over append-only ledger transitions, "the same proof shape" credited to bond conservation (L640-642). |
| RP-005 | Sec.2 Tier A -- A4 Canary detection power + sequential leak testing (p.2) | development | 1-beta^k detection for a k-canary leak plus a Wald SPRT giving expected time-to-detection; re-scopes the exfiltration bond to funding detection since breach damage is unbounded. | re-spine chapter 6 (The Sealed Harbor); research paper 4 |  | DONE 7fbb9c64f | thm:sealed-power/thm:sealed-latency (L678,688) give 1-beta^k detection and Wald SPRT expected time; L738-742 re-scopes the exfiltration bond to "funding detection and response," not unbounded breach damage. |

| CPR-014 | Sec.2.1 Solution 1 (6.1) -- why read-poverty binds (p.4) | exercise-key | The write side already has mature primitives (claims, uniqueness, locks, merge queues); the read side (find the right agent/work/collaborator) has no primitive, so legible state accumulates faster than it can be consulted. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 4f4abca72 | def:readpoverty pullquote (l.1519-1522): 'The write side has known fixes -- claims, locks, merge queues... The read side has been left implicit.' |
| CPR-015 | Sec.2.1 Solution 2 (6.2) -- write-time cost vs. read-time speed (p.4) | exercise-key | Every registration/claim/note/episode update must also maintain the directory index (write-time O(log n) or O(1) cost); queries then answer 'who/what matches q' in O(query) instead of scanning O(n) participants. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 4f4abca72 | ex:ls-directory-database-analogy (l.1834-1835): write-time O(log n)/O(1) index maintenance buys O(log n+k) selective reads instead of O(n) scanning. |
| CPR-016 | Sec.2.1 Solution 3 (6.3) -- discovery is vacuous at n=1 (p.4) | exercise-key | At n=1 the session briefing, honest-attestation/completion gate, and footgun guard already reduce uncertainty; none is discovery-ranking because with a single agent there is no candidate set to rank. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 035376990 | ex:ls-value-curve-n1 (l.1841-1842): at n=1 briefing/attestation/footgun-gating/completion/resurrection already pay off and none of them is discovery ranking. |
| CPR-017 | Sec.2.1 Solution 4 (6.4) -- the digest floor at the extremes (p.4) | exercise-key | As k->0 the floor vanishes (a mostly-safe swarm is cheap to digest); as k->N/2 the floor grows to Theta(N) bits for any fixed review budget m<<N, so past a load-bearing density 'summarize' degenerates to 'list' -- the formal justification for keeping the dangerous set small by construction. | The Legible Swarm; research paper 1 |  | DONE 035376990 | ex:ls-floor-limits (l.1849-1850) gives both endpoints: floor to 0 as k to 0, floor to ~N bits at k=N/2 for fixed m<<N -- the formal case for keeping the dangerous set small. |
| CPR-018 | Sec.2.1 Solution 5 (6.5, starred) -- adversarial load-bearing estimation (p.4) | exercise-key | Against an adaptive adversary the bound becomes log2 C(N,k') with k' the indistinguishability-class size; the adversary attacks by inflation (drive the floor toward Theta(N), a legibility denial-of-service) or mimicry (make critical artifacts appear inert); the defense is building the digest only from signals the agent cannot forge, with the residue an inspection game sharing Conjecture III.11.1's Becker condition. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE b81eb4794 | ex:ls-adversarial-critical-set (l.1858-1859) covers the general adversarial argument but omits the log2 C(N,k') refinement, the named inflation/mimicry attacks, and the Becker-condition link.; ex:ls-adversarial-critical-set gives the log2 C(N,k') refinement, names inflation and mimicry, and links to the Becker condition |
| CPR-019 | Sec.2.2 Solution 6 (7.1) -- no monotone transform unifies discovery and attention (p.4-5) | exercise-key | Discovery maximizes capability fit (Eq I.2); the attention queue maximizes regret-if-ignored=stakes*irreversibility*anomaly (Eq I.3); high-fit/low-risk and low-fit/high-risk items order oppositely under the two, so no strictly monotone scalar transform can identify them. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 4f4abca72 | thm:split-ranker (l.1962-1991) and ex:ls-split-ranker-statement (l.2198-2199) state the opposite-ordering proof exactly (high-fit/low-risk vs low-fit/high-risk items). |
| CPR-020 | Sec.2.2 Solution 7 (7.2) -- why the attention queue is easier to game (p.5) | exercise-key | The attention queue's inputs are self-influenceable and its miss-cost asymmetry forces it to err toward surfacing, subsidizing manufactured distress; the discovery head is anchored in costly-to-forge pull signals whose prize (a routing you cannot serve) is self-punishing. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 035376990 | ex:ls-goodhart-exposure (l.2205-2206): attention queue is self-influenceable with an irreversible-loss asymmetry; discovery is anchored in pull signals, self-punishing to game. |
| CPR-021 | Sec.2.2 Solution 8 (7.3) -- Prop I.7.1's role in unblocking a dead claimant (p.5) | exercise-key | Without the proposition a dead agent's claim row persists as 'current' forever, denying a live agent; with a class-specific half-life and fencing epoch the stale row decays out of 'current' and the live agent unblocks at its next claim attempt. | The Single-Writer Kernel; The Legible Swarm |  | DONE b6a363ae0 | needs adjudication: spans multiple future waves (wave 2, wave 5); author should decide which one resolves it.; landed per checked whitepaper/single-writer-kernel.tex, whitepaper/legible-swarm.tex — `whitepaper/single-writer-kernel.tex:462` ("current"): The communication o |
| CPR-022 | Sec.2.2 Solution 9 (7.4) -- pricing dismissed escalations without silencing minorities (p.5) | exercise-key | The daemon debits escalation standing only on a confirmed dismissed escalation, not on escalation per se, so genuine distress survives while crying wolf carries negative expected value unless the wolf is usually real. | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 035376990 | ex:ls-costly-signal-debit (l.2219-2220): the debit fires only on a confirmed dismissed escalation, never solely because the operator clicked dismiss. |
| CPR-023 | Sec.2.2 Solution 10 (7.5, starred) -- implicit relevance feedback and its two Goodhart channels (p.5) | exercise-key | Accept/reject can train the ranker via learning-to-rank, but the loop optimizes for operator acceptance (legibility-pleasing self-presentation) and suffers selection bias (only surfaced candidates get feedback); the fix trains on witnessed downstream outcome, not click, making Chapter III's substrate the correct training signal for Chapter I's ranker. | The Legible Swarm; From Spawn to Person |  | DONE 4f4abca72 | needs adjudication: spans multiple future waves (wave 5, wave 6); author should decide which one resolves it.; landed per checked whitepaper/legible-swarm.tex, website-v2/public/whitepaper/spawn-to-person.tex — `website-v2/public/whitepaper/spawn-to-person.tex:391` ("III" |
| CPR-024 | Sec.2.2 Solution 11 (7.6, starred) -- decay vs. summary, a decision rule (p.5) | exercise-key | Classify each signal by reconstructability, decision-relevance horizon, and write frequency: decay high-frequency reconstructable coordination traces, summarize low-frequency non-reconstructable decisions, evict-plus-pointer for reconstructable artifacts -- 'never summarize what you can decay; never decay what you cannot reconstruct.' | The Legible Swarm |  | DONE 035376990 | ex:ls-decay-vs-summary (l.2236-2237) classifies by reconstructability/relevance/frequency into hard-expiry, state-transition, summary+provenance, and evict+pointer, matching the five-way split. |

## Exposition-review corpus -- `docs/harbor-research/exposition-review/CROSS-DOCUMENT-SYNTHESIS.md` and the fourteen `*-notes.md` files in that directory

Rows are grouped by the notes file they come from, named in each row's `Section / page` cell (e.g. `paper1-notes.md`, `CROSS-DOCUMENT-SYNTHESIS.md`). The synthesis document's own section B ("systemic patterns") rows are `XS-028` through `XS-043`.

## Duplicates and overlaps across sources

The six PDF memoranda were written in sequence, each building on the last, and the exposition
review corpus was produced independently over the same underlying texts -- so the same
underlying issue is frequently raised more than once, in different words, by different
documents. This section groups the clearest recurring clusters so the author can adjudicate
each cluster once rather than once per row. It is a starting point, not exhaustive; many more
individual cross-references are noted inline in affected rows' `Prior adjudication` cells.

| Cluster | Row ids | What recurs |
|---|---|---|
| SDT direction/objective reversed | HAH-012, HAH-028, RT-004, PD-008, RP-003 | Figure I.6's criterion direction and possible attention double-count; RP-003 is the theorem that closes it. |
| Exact-key uniqueness is not mutual exclusion, plus the missing fencing epoch | HAH-014, HAH-036, RT-002, PD-008 | Theorem II.6.1 scope; same fix (canonical conflict predicate + epoch) proposed everywhere. |
| WAL auto-checkpoint does not bound elapsed loss time | HAH-015, HAH-035, RT-007, PD-008 | Page-count threshold vs. a timer; same durability-by-fault-class fix. |
| "Reference monitor" overstates the same-user design; the five assurance levels | HAH-016, RT-008, PD-007, PD-008 | Same five-level scale (Observed/Coordinated/Brokered/Confined/Attested) proposed independently by RT and HAH, adopted wholesale by PD. |
| Non-forgeable-identity theorem false as stated; principal-level aggregation as the fix | HAH-017, HAH-047, RT-003, PD-008, CPR-029, CPR-030, CPR-031 | Same counterexample (mature-10/sanction-2/newcomer-0) and the same principal-binding repair, independently re-derived in the exercise-key and CPR solution sets. |
| Float-plan fund/collateral confusion, four-bucket fix | HAH-018, HAH-056, RT-005, PD-008 | Requester escrow vs. provider bond vs. insurer reserve vs. fees. |
| Anchor attenuation: compare every edge, not node-to-root; Kani scoping | HAH-019, HAH-065, HAH-066, RT-006, PD-008 | The write-then-read-then-write escalation attack and the relational-proof requirement. |
| Stage game is not a Prisoner's Dilemma; wrong repeated-game threshold | HAH-020, HAH-068, RT-001 | Same payoff-matrix correction and delta-threshold re-derivation. |
| Cryptography cannot prove reality; six typed evidence channels | HAH-022, RT-008, PD-008 | Identical channel taxonomy proposed in both HAH and RT. |
| Rate-the-raters / inspection tower / rho\*=G/(dB) | HAH-052, RT-012, RP-009, PP-003, CPR-008, CPR-034, XS-008, XS-137, XS-138, XS-300, XS-301 | Converts Conjecture III.11.1 into a parameterized theorem; independently re-derived by the CPR solution set and flagged as understated in the whitepaper by two different exposition reviewers. |
| "JSON ledger IS the neural state" overclaim | RT-010, PD-017, PD-025, HAH-092 | Same retraction (sufficient statistic for same-provider continuation, not identity) proposed by RT and PD; HAH supplies the impossibility argument independently. |
| B2B airlock: control the channel not the token; two declassification gates | RT-011, PD-004, PD-024, HAH-103, HAH-104, CPR-056, CPR-057, CPR-058, CPR-059, CPR-060, RP-018, PP-004 | The same clean-room architecture (whole-worker taint, two gates, information-theoretic leakage budget) independently converged on by RT, HAH's Sealed Harbor section, and CPR's Part 5, then formalized as research paper 4 in RP/PP. |
| Inception Canaries: replace live-vulnerability injection with consented, blinded historical-defect probes | RT-013, PD-026 | Identical fix in both documents. |
| Re-spine the volume into an 8-chapter work-unit-first structure | RT-015 through RT-022, HAH-120 through HAH-127 | Near-identical chapter list and ordering proposed independently by RT's Move 1 and HAH's "Proposed new volume." |
| Effect hypervisor = Ramadge-Wonham controllability | HAH-089, RP-010, PP-002, CPR-044, XS-047, XS-296, XS-297 | Same theorem (research paper 2 / RP's B3) approached from the product memo, the research roadmap, the CPR mechanism review, and independently flagged as an uncredited cross-reference in the single-writer-kernel/paper2 exposition-review pair. |
| Work-unit transition system as the volume's formal spine | HAH-110, RP-017 | HAH names it qualitatively; RP's C0 gives it the same six safety properties. |
| Engine substitution / (principal, engine)-indexed reputation | HAH-093, RP-012, PP-005, CPR-047, PD-012, PD-020 | Same lemons-market attack and attestation fix, from the local-harbor build sequence through to the formal paper. |
| Front-loaded probation cliff, and its later feasibility-cap correction | RP-013, PP-005, XS-017, XS-175, XS-192, XS-198, XS-019 | RP/PP state the original theorem; the exposition-review corpus (paper5-notes and its cross-reference to spawn-to-person) independently finds the same uniqueness claim retracted in one place and still asserted in five others. |
| Tractable deontic-conflict fragment for "who decides what is a conflict" | HAH-096, RP-011, PP-006, CPR-051 | Same polynomial-fragment result from the local-harbor design, the research roadmap, and the CPR condominium-harbor chapter. |
| Adversarial test market / assurance as a purchasable (1-d)^k line item | HAH-094, PD-011, RP-021 through RP-029, CPR-048, XS-431 through XS-434 | Same mechanism proposed in HAH's GitHub review fleet, PD's Phase 3, CPR's Mechanism 9, and flagged in the harbor-economy exposition review as the chapter's one orphaned, untagged, unbacked "Mechanism 9." |
